# NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) Physics - Oscillations

## Topic 3: Time Period and Frequency

**Total Questions:** 57

### Q1. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

If the displacement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>)</mo></math> of a particle executing SHM are related through the expression <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>. If the time period of the particle is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt></math>, then what is the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math>?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **12**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given equation,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
Differentiating both sides, we get
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi>v</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>v</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>x</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mo>&#8757;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>v</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><msqrt><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mtext>&#160;acceleration&#160;</mtext><mtext>&#160;displacement&#160;</mtext></mfrac></mfenced></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><msqrt><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfenced></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mn>12</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"></math></p>

</details>

### Q2. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle executes simple harmonic motion and it is located at <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>b</mi></math> and <math><mi>c</mi></math> at time <math><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi> </mi><mn>3</mn><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> respectively. The frequency of the oscillation is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></math>...<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<math><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>...<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<math><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>...<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>adding equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<math><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>A</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></math>...<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>sin</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mn>2</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>D</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>D</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>from equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>b</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<math><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></math></p>

</details>

### Q3. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

As shown in the figure, a particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>gm</mi></math> is attached with four identical springs, each of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. Initial tension in each spring is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>. Neglecting gravity, calculate the angular frequency (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) of small oscillations of the particle along a line perpendicular to the plane of the figure.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ph-28.png" style="width: 200px; height: 160px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **100**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ph-28-1.png" style="width: 247px; height: 120px;" /></p><p>For small <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> we can assume the tension in each spring to remain constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>&#8776;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>x</mi></mrow><mi>l</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>x</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
 </p>

</details>

### Q4. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider one dimensional motion of a particle of mass <math> <mi>m</mi> </math>. It has potential energy <math><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, where <math> <mi>a</mi> </math> and <math> <mi>b</mi> </math> are positive constants. At origin <math><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo></math> it has initial velocity <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>V</mi> <mn>0 </mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>. It performs simple harmonic oscillations. The frequency of the simple harmonic motion depends on

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>b</mi></math> and <math><mi>m</mi></math> alone
- **(B)** <math><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>a</mi></math>  and  <math><mi>m</mi></math>  alone
- **(C)** <math><mi>b</mi></math>  alone
- **(D)** <math><mi>b</mi></math>  and  <math><mi>a</mi></math>  alone

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mi>b</mi></math> and <math><mi>m</mi></math> alone**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given,

Potential energy, <math><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

Force, <math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mi>b</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>.</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi><mi>x</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>x</mi></math> .......(i)

Compare equation. (i) with the standard relation of displacement in case of simple harmonic motion.

i.e., <math><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>x</mi></math>

i.e., <math><msup><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mi>ω</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

i.e., the frequency of the simple harmonic motion depends on <math><mi>b</mi></math> and <math><mi>m</mi></math> alone.

</details>

### Q5. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle executes simple harmonic motion with a time period of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> along the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> - axis. At time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>, the particle crosses the mean position while at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>, the velocity of the particle is  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mo> </mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The amplitude of motion (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>) is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>π</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>16</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>π</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>24</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>π</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>32</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mi>π</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>32</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mi>π</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The general equation of SHM is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, here</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>16</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>, the particle is at the mean position. Hence,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>So the equation of SHM will become</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"/></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>32</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q6. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The length of a simple pendulum executing simple harmonic motion is increased by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>21</mn><mo>%</mo></math>. The percentage increase in the time period of the pendulum of increased length is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>11</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>21</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>42</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>%</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>%</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let the lengths of the pendulum be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mtext>100</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext><mtext>)</mtext></math>  and  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mtext>121</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Since we know the time period of a simple pendulum is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi>l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math>        <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mtext>T</mtext><mo>'</mo></msup><mtext>T</mtext></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>121</mn><mtext>100</mtext></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math>        Fractional change = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mtext>T</mtext><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>-</mo><mtext>T</mtext></mrow><mtext>T</mtext></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>11</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math>        Percentage change = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q7. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A horizontal platform, with an object placed on it, is executing SHM in the vertical direction. The amplitude of oscillation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2.5</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi></math>. Let the least period of these oscillations, so that the object is not detached, be equal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>π</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>. Then, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math> is (Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

The object would not leave the platform when 
<math><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>g</mi></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><msup><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>A</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>g</mi></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>w</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2.5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>25</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>20</mn><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mo>⇒</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q8. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A uniform solid cylinder can roll without sliding on a horizontal surface as shown in the figure. The mass of cylinder is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> and the spring constant for both the ideal springs is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. What is the angular frequency (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>rad</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) of small oscillations of the centre of the cylinder?

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/Phys_22.JPG" style="width: 250px; height: 129px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The total energy of the system is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>x</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q9. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The system shown in the adjacent figure is in equilibrium. The mass of the container with liquid is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math>, the density of the liquid in the container is <math><mi>ρ</mi></math> and volume of the block is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math>. If the container is now displaced downwards through a distance <math><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> and released such that block remains well inside the liquid, during subsequent motion (ignore viscosity)

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/lyhbzfrsipsldkyjfbjvvqpsypdobbqfsetidyqjxnpnzcijng.jpeg" style="height: 224px; width: 150px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** Time period of SHM of the container will be <math><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+2</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** Time period of SHM of the container will be <math><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** The amplitude of SHM of the container will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** Container will not perform SHM

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Time period of SHM of the container will be <math><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi> </mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>M</mi><mi>a</mi></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/WhatsApp_Image_2020-07-21_at_3.07.43_PM.jpeg" style="width: 200px; height: 210px;" />

Where <math><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi>V</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>x</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>x</mi></math>

Or <math><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math> or <math><mi>ω</mi><mo>=</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

The amplitude of SHM will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

</details>

---

### Q10. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A rod of mass M and length L is hinged at its one end and carries a block of mass m at its lower end. A spring of force constant k<sub>1</sub> is installed at distance a from the hinge and another of force constant k<sub>2</sub> at a distance b as shown in the figure. If the whole arrangement rests on a smooth horizontal table top, find the frequency of vibration.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/fdhgdfhdghdofil.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 280px; height: 182px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>1</mn>   <mrow>    <mn>2</mn><mi>&#x03C0;</mi></mrow>  </mfrac>  <msqrt>   <mrow>    <mfrac>     <mrow>      <msub>       <mi>k</mi>       <mn>1</mn>      </msub>      <msup>       <mi>a</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mo>+</mo><msub>       <mi>k</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msub>      <msup>       <mi>b</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>     <mrow>      <msup>       <mi>L</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mrow><mo>(</mo>       <mrow>        <mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac>         <mi>M</mi>         <mn>3</mn>        </mfrac>        </mrow>      <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow>    </mfrac>    </mrow>  </msqrt>  </mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>1</mn>   <mrow>    <mn>2</mn><mi>&#x03C0;</mi></mrow>  </mfrac>  <msqrt>   <mrow>    <mfrac>     <mrow>      <msub>       <mi>k</mi>       <mn>1</mn>      </msub>      <msup>       <mi>a</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mo>+</mo><msub>       <mi>k</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msub>      <msup>       <mi>b</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>     <mrow>      <msup>       <mi>L</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mrow><mo>(</mo>       <mrow>        <mi>m</mi><mo>&#x2212;</mo><mfrac>         <mi>M</mi>         <mn>3</mn>        </mfrac>        </mrow>      <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow>    </mfrac>    </mrow>  </msqrt>  </mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>1</mn>   <mrow>    <mn>2</mn><mi>&#x03C0;</mi></mrow>  </mfrac>  <msqrt>   <mrow>    <mfrac>     <mrow>      <msub>       <mi>k</mi>       <mn>1</mn>      </msub>      <msup>       <mi>a</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mo>&#x2212;</mo><msub>       <mi>k</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msub>      <msup>       <mi>b</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>     <mrow>      <msup>       <mi>L</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mrow><mo>(</mo>       <mrow>        <mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac>         <mi>M</mi>         <mn>3</mn>        </mfrac>        </mrow>      <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow>    </mfrac>    </mrow>  </msqrt>  </mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>1</mn>   <mrow>    <mn>4</mn><mi>&#x03C0;</mi></mrow>  </mfrac>  <msqrt>   <mrow>    <mfrac>     <mrow>      <msub>       <mi>k</mi>       <mn>1</mn>      </msub>      <msup>       <mi>a</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mo>+</mo><msub>       <mi>k</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msub>      <msup>       <mi>b</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>     <mrow>      <msup>       <mi>L</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mrow><mo>(</mo>       <mrow>        <mi>m</mi><mo>&#x2212;</mo><mfrac>         <mi>M</mi>         <mn>3</mn>        </mfrac>        </mrow>      <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow>    </mfrac>    </mrow>  </msqrt>  </mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>1</mn>   <mrow>    <mn>2</mn><mi>&#x03C0;</mi></mrow>  </mfrac>  <msqrt>   <mrow>    <mfrac>     <mrow>      <msub>       <mi>k</mi>       <mn>1</mn>      </msub>      <msup>       <mi>a</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mo>+</mo><msub>       <mi>k</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msub>      <msup>       <mi>b</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      </mrow>     <mrow>      <msup>       <mi>L</mi>       <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mrow><mo>(</mo>       <mrow>        <mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac>         <mi>M</mi>         <mn>3</mn>        </mfrac>        </mrow>      <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow>    </mfrac>    </mrow>  </msqrt>  </mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

For small angular displacement θ, net torque towards mean position is

<math> <mrow> <mi>τ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mtext>a</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>b</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> </math>     or     <math> <mrow> <mtext>I</mtext> <mi>α</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>a</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>b</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

or        <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>mL</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>ML</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>α</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>a</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>b</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

or        <math> <mrow> <mi>α</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext><mn>1</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>a</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>b</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>L</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

∴        <math> <mrow> <msup> <mi>ω</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>a</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>b</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>L</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014754_AnsExp1_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 381px; height: 272px;" />

Hence, frequency

<math> <mrow> <mtext>f</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>a</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>k</mtext><mn>2</mn> </msub> <msup> <mtext>b</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>L</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q11. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The length of the second's pendulum on the surface of the earth is <math><mn>1 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. If the acceleration due to gravity on the surface of the moon is one-sixth of the acceleration due to gravity of the earth, then the length of the second's pendulum on the surface of the moon will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6 </mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>6 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>36 </mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>36 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6 </mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Seconds pendulum is a special pendulum having a time period of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>.

Using the formula <math> <mrow> <mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>L</mi> <mi>g</mi></mfrac></mrow></msqrt></mrow></math>

If the time period remains the same and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi></math> is reduced by a factor of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn></math>.
L should be reduced by a factor of 6.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> on moon.

</details>

---

### Q12. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The time period of a spring-mass system is <math><mi>T</mi></math>. If this spring is cut into two parts, whose lengths are in the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, and the same mass is attached to the longer part, the new time period will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mi> </mi><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mi>T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The spring-mass system oscillates in SHM. Its time period is given by</p><p><math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math></p><p>When spring is cut in ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, the new spring constant is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>'</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>&#8203;/2</mo></math> (for the longer part)</p><p><math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>T</mi><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>'</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mi>k</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math></p><p><math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>'</mo><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mi>T</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q13. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider the figure here. A particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is constrained to move inside a smooth vertical groove of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> and is connected to a light spring of spring constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> in equilibrium. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> is centre of the groove. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></math> are horizontal and vertical axes respectively. Different physical parameters are related as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>KR</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>mg</mi></math>. Angular frequency of the oscillations, if the particle is slightly displaced from the shown equilibrium position is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/137114-2.JPG" style="width: 230px; height: 419px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Restoring torque on small displacement  <math><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></math> 

 <math><mi mathvariant="normal">τ</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></math> 

 <math><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">τ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math> 

 <math><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></math> 

 <math><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

</details>

### Q14. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What will be the time period of the pendulum of length l suspended from the ceiling of a cart which is sliding without friction on an inclined plane of inclination <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></math> ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>&#960;</mtext> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mtext>&#952;</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>π</mtext> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow><mn>3</mn> <mi>l</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mtext>θ</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mtext>&#960;</mtext> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow><mn>2</mn> <mi>l</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mtext>&#952;</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mtext>π</mtext> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow><mn>4</mn> <mi>l</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>2g</mtext> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mtext>θ</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>&#960;</mtext> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mtext>&#952;</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Here, point of suspension has an acceleration. <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>&#8594;</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math> = g sin  &theta; (down the plane). Further, <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>g</mtext> <mo>&#8594;</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math> can be resolved into two components g sin  &theta; (along the plane) and g cos  &theta; (perpendicular to plane).

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014074_AnsExp1_1.jpg" /><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014074_AnsExp1_2.jpg" />
<math><mrow><msub><mrow><mover accent="true"><mtext>g</mtext> <mo>&#8594;</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mtext>eff</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>g</mtext> <mo>&#8594;</mo> </mover> <mo>-</mo> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>&#8594;</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math>

= g cos&theta; (perpendicular to plane)

<math><mrow><mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>&#960;</mtext> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>|</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>g</mtext> <mo>&#8594;</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mtext>eff</mtext> </msub> <mo>|</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

    <math>&#160;<mrow><mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>&#960;</mtext> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> <mtext>cos</mtext><mtext>&#952;</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q15. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

At a certain place, the acceleration due to gravity is determined using a simple pendulum. The length of the pendulum is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> and a stopwatch with least count of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> measures the time taken by the pendulum for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn></math> oscillations to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>. The accuracy in the measurement of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>%</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>%</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#916;g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>&#916;L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mfrac><mi>&#916;g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#916;T</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>&#916;L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mfrac><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>50</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>25</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>%</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace></math>

</details>

---

### Q16. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A highly rigid cubical block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> of small mass <math><mi>M</mi></math> and side <math><mi>L</mi></math> is fixed rigidly on to another cubical block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> of the same dimensions and of low modulus of rigidity <math><mi>η</mi></math> such that the lower face of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> completely covers the upper face of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>. The lower face of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is rigidly held on a horizontal surface. A small force <math><mi>F</mi></math> is applied perpendicular to one of the side faces of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>. After the force is withdrawn, block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> executes small oscillations, the time period of which is given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>η</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow></msqrt></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>η</mi></mrow><mi>L</mi></mfrac></mrow></msqrt></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><mi>η</mi></mfrac></mrow></msqrt></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>η</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msqrt></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>η</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msqrt></mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Modulus of rigidity,<math> <mrow> <mi>η</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mi>F</mi> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mo>.</mo><mtext> </mtext><mi>θ</mi></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> Here, <math> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><msup> <mi>L</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

And

<math> <mrow> <mi>θ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>L</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
 
Therefore, restoring force is <math> <mrow> <mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mi>η</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>A</mi><mi>θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mi>η</mi><mi>L</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow> </math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0007491_Ques4_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 290px; height: 218px;" />
 
Or acceleration, <math> <mrow> <mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mi>F</mi> <mi>M</mi> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mi>η</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow> <mi>M</mi> </mfrac> <mi>x</mi></mrow> </math>

Since, <math> <mrow> <mi>a</mi><mo>∝</mo><mi>x</mi> </mrow> </math>, oscillations are simaple harmonic in nature, time period of which is given by

<math> <mrow> <mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt> <mrow> <mrow><mo>|</mo> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>p</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow> <mrow> <mi>a</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>n</mi></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>|</mo></mrow></mrow> </msqrt> <mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt> <mrow> <mrow><mo>|</mo> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>x</mi> <mi>a</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>|</mo></mrow></mrow> </msqrt> <mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>M</mi> <mrow> <mi>η</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q17. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two identical balls <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, each of mass<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math>, are attached to two identical massless springs. The spring-mass system is constrained to move inside a rigid smooth pipe bent in the form of a circle as shown in the figure. The pipe is fixed in a horizontal plane. The centres of the balls can move in a circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. Each spring has a natural length of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and spring constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, Initially, both the balls are displaced by an angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>θ</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>π</mi><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo> </mo><mi>rad</mi></math> with respect to the diameter<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mi>PQ</mi><mo> </mo></math>of the circle (as shown in the figure) and released from rest.
The frequency of oscillation of balls is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0007521_Ques1_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="height: 171px; width: 250px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>&pi;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext> Hz</mtext></mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mi>&pi;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext> Hz</mtext></mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext> Hz</mtext> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mi>&pi;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext> Hz</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext> Hz</mtext> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, Mass of each block A and B, m = 0.1 kg

Radius of circle, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/Ques_7521_1_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 250px; height: 199px;" />

The natural length of spring <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>0.06</mtext> <mi>π</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mi>π</mi> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math> (Half circle) and spring constant,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

In the stretched position elongation in each spring

<math> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>R</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

Let us draw FBD of A

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/Ques_7521_1_2.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 250px; height: 220px;" />

Spring in lower side is stretched by 2x and on upper side compressed by 2x. Therefore, a force 2kx on each block would be exerted by each spring.

Hence, a restoring force, F = 4kx will act on A in the direction shown in figure.

Restoring torque of this force about origin

<math> <mrow> <mi>τ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>F. R</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mtext>kx</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mtext>kR</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

or <math> <mrow> <mi>τ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>4</mn> <msup> <mtext>kR</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>·</mo> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>   ..... (i)

Since, <math> <mrow> <mi>τ</mi> <mo>∝</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>, each ball executes angular SHM about origin O.

Equation. (i) can be written as

<math> <mrow> <mtext>I</mtext> <mi>α</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>4</mn> <msup> <mtext>kR</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

or   <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>mR</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>α</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>4</mn> <msup> <mtext>kR</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

or <math> <mrow> <mi>α</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

Frequency of oscillation, <math> <mrow> <mtext>f</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfenced close="|" open="|" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>acceleration</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>displacement</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

                                         <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfenced close="|" open="|" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>α</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

                                       <math> <mrow> <mtext>f</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

Substituting the values, we have

<math> <mrow> <mtext>f</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mo>×</mo> <mtext>0.1</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>0.1</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext> Hz</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q18. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A rod of mass <math><mi>M</mi></math> and length <math><mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi></math> is performing SHM as torsional pendulum in the horizontal plane. Two blocks, each of mass <math><mi>m</mi></math>, are put at distance <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> from the centre. The frequency after putting blocks of mass <math><mi>m</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">%</mi></math> of initial frequency. Then, the ratio of <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> will be
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/WhatsApp_Image_2020-06-24_at_6.49.53_PM.jpeg" style="width: 180px; height: 170px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>12</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>14</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>16</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>18</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>16</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mi>C</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mfrac><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0.2</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mi>C</mi><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>M</mi><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mi>&#160;</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>0.04</mn><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>12</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mn>4</mn><mi>M</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.16</mn><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0.24</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>M</mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3.84</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0.24</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn></math>

</details>

### Q19. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

There is a cylindrical bottle of negligible mass and radius <math><mn>2.5</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> floating in the water of density <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> filled with <math><mn>310</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></math> of water inside it. Now the bottle is slightly dipped into water and released. The frequency of oscillation is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>3.75 </mn><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi> </mi></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>1.25 </mn><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi> </mi></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>2.75 </mn><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi> </mi></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>3.00 </mn><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi> </mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>1.25 </mn><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi> </mi></mrow></mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let at equilibrium, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math> length of the bottle is immersed in water:-

So, <math><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>b</mi><mi>u</mi><mi>o</mi></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#961;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>g</mi></math> ..... <math><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math> [Equilibrium Condition]

Now it is further pushed by distance <math><mi>&#8242;</mi><mi>x</mi><mi>&#8242;</mi></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#961;</mi><mo>[</mo><mi>A</mi><mfenced close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced><mi>g</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></math>

<math><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>x</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>y</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></math>

<math><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>f</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>g</mi></mrow><mi>V</mi></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.25</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math>

</details>
