### Q29. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A weightless rigid rod with a small iron bob at the end is hinged at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> by a ball and socket joint to the wall so that it can rotate in all directions. The rod is kept in the horizontal position by a vertical inextensible string of length<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi></math>, fixed at its midpoint. The bob is displaced slightly, perpendicular to the plane of the figure. If the period of small oscillations of the system is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mi>π</mi></mrow><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>, then what is the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>? [Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>]
<img alt="Picture 171" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/544826.PNG" style="width: 200px; height: 90px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The small oscillation of the bob occur relative to a stationary axis passing through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math>. The line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CD</mi></math> is perpendicular to the axis <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math>. The bob behaves as a simple pendulum of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CD</mi></math> oscillating due to component of its weight <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math>.</p><p>
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/image21.jpg" style="width: 220px; height: 144px;" /></p><p>If the length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AD</mi></math> is equal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math>, then the length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CD</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>L</mi><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>tan</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mi>g</mi></mfrac></msqrt><mi>&#160;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>l</mi></mrow><mi>g</mi></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>10</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q30. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle of mass <math><mi>m</mi></math> is performing the linear simple harmonic motion. Its equilibrium is at <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, force constant is <math><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> and amplitude of SHM is <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A.</mi></math> The maximum power supplied by the restoring force to the particle during SHM will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mi>m</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mi>m</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mi>m</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mi>m</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mi>m</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Power supplied by the restoring force <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is:
<math><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>V</mi></math>
<math><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>K</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow></msub><msqrt><msup><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></math>
<math><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><msqrt><msup><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi></mi></msqrt></math>
<math><mi>P</mi></math> <em>will be max when</em> <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math><mo>⇒</mo><msup><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><msub><mrow><mo>|</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi></mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><msqrt><msup><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mi>m</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q31. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is constrained to move along with a moveable pulley of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> which is connected to a spring of force constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math>, as shown in the figure. If the mass of the fixed pulley is negligible and friction is absent everywhere, then the period of small oscillations of the system is
<u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014752_Ques1_1.jpg" style="width: 203px; height: 257px;" /></strong></u></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mtext>k</mtext></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

If the mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is displaced by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> and has speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></math>, spring will extend by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> will have speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></math>. Energy of this system can be written as

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>gx</mtext><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><msup><mtext>v</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mtext>g</mtext><mfrac><mtext>x</mtext><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mtext>v</mtext><mo>⁡</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mtext>x</mtext><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>const.</mtext></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>gx</mtext><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><msup><mtext>v</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mtext>gx</mtext><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mtext>v</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msup><mtext>x</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>const.</mtext></math>

Differentiate wrt time

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>gv</mtext><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>v</mtext><mfrac><mtext>dv</mtext><mtext>dt</mtext></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mtext>g</mtext></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mtext>v</mtext><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mtext>v</mtext><mfrac><mtext>dv</mtext><mtext>dt</mtext></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mtext>x.v</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mo>-</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>g</mtext><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mfrac><mtext>dv</mtext><mtext>dt</mtext></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mtext>g</mtext></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mfrac><mtext>dv</mtext><mtext>dt</mtext></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mtext>x</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014752_AnsExp1_1.jpg" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mfrac><mtext>dv</mtext><mtext>dt</mtext></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mtext>x</mtext><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>g</mtext><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mtext>g</mtext></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext>dv</mtext><mtext>dt</mtext></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mtext>x</mtext><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mtext>g</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

Here,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mtext>T</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>M</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>

</details>

---

### Q32. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A simple pendulum is taken to<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mn>64</mn><mo> </mo><mi>km</mi></math> above the earth's surface. It's time period will :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Increase by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(B)** Decrease by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(C)** Increase by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(D)** Decrease by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>%</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Increase by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>%</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi>l</mi><mi>g</mi></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>l</mi><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>h</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>h</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

or  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>T</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mi>T</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>h</mi></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>h</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>T</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mi>T</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>T</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><mi>T</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math> : Time period will increase by 1%

</details>

---

### Q33. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The time period of a physical pendulum about some pivot point is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math>. When we take another pivot point, opposite of the first one such that the centre of mass of the physical pendulum lies on the line joining these two pivot points, we obtain the same time period. If the two points are separated by a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math>, then the time period <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#x3C0;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>g</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#x3C0;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>g</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#x3C0;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#x3C0;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi></mrow><mi>g</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#x3C0;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi>L</mi><mi>g</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo></math> <math><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>x</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>x</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

</details>

---

### Q34. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

When a string is divided into three segments of lengths <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext><mn>3</mn></msub></math> the fundamental frequencies of these three segments are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> respectively. The original fundamental frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi></mfenced></math> of the string is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></msqrt><mo>+</mo><msqrt><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msqrt><mo>+</mo><msqrt><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></msqrt></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext></math> be the length of the string.

The fundamental frequency is given by

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>or</mtext><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo></mrow></mfrac></math> ( <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></math> are constant )  

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>or</mtext><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo></mrow></mfrac></math> ( where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math> is a constant )

Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi></mfrac></math>

But <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext><mn>3</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q35. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The time period of oscillations of a block attached to a spring is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>. When the spring is replaced by another spring, the time period of the block is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. If both the springs are connected in series and the block is made to oscillate using the combination, then the time period of the block is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>T</mtext><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mtext>T</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mtext>T</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>1</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mtext>T</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>1</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mtext>T</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

When springs are in series, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math> 

For  first  spring, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mtext>m</mtext><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></msqrt></math>

For  Second  spring, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mtext>m</mtext><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴</mo> </mrow> </math>         <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext>m</mtext></mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext>m</mtext></mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext>m</mtext><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>

or          <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mtext>m</mtext><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

or           <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msup><mtext>T</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup></math> .

</details>

---

### Q36. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A cylindrical piston of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math> slides smoothly inside a long cylinder closed at one end, enclosing a certain mass of gas. The cylinder is kept with its axis horizontal. If the piston is disturbed from its equilibrium position, it oscillates simple harmonically. The period of oscillation will be (Assume isothermal process)
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/944a85e3-f42b-4cda-9c32-1e4291230961.png" style="width:1.76042in;height:1.26042in" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msqrt><mi>M</mi><mi>P</mi><mi>h</mi><mi>A</mi></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let the piston be displaced through distance <math><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> towards left, then volume decreases, pressure increases. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>P</mi></math> is an increase in pressure and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>V</mi></math> is a decrease in volume, then considering the process to take place slowly (i.e. Isothermal)
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/431645e3-9228-4c63-9009-27c9d485201e.png" style="width:1.68681in;height:1.34028in" />
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>⇒</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>P</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>ΔV</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>V</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>P</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>P</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>P</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mi>h</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mi>h</mi></mfrac><mi>x</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ω</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q37. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A cylinder of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> lies on a plank of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> as shown. The surface between plank and ground is smooth, and between cylinder and plank is rough. Assuming no slipping between cylinder and plank, the time period of oscillations (when displaced from equilibrium) of the system is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image12-b4017cbc-1092-4d2b-8145-3ecb37b3ab4e.png" style="height: 96px; width: 250px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using no Slipping condition:

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image13-95128195-ed98-4a82-a850-9c4a65989d0e.jpg" style="height: 127px; width: 250px;" />

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub><mo>⇒</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

</details>

---

### Q38. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One end of a spring of force constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi></math> is fixed to a vertical wall and other to a body of mass <i><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math></i> resting on smooth horizontal surface. There is another wall at a distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> from the body. If all the collisions are elastic and spring is compressed by <math><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> and released, then the time period of oscillation is

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/wwwktltxiydigczhrmiepfjkyxiitzrymtqfdusotwgsbpzwmx.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math> 

 <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q39. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the time period of the seconds pendulum, on a planet similar to earth, but mass and radius three times as that of the earth?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2 </mn><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>6 </mn><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo></msqrt><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo></msqrt><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi>l</mi><mi>g</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></msub></math>

Time period at earth for seconds pendulum <math><mo>=</mo><mn>2 </mn><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#x2234;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo></msqrt><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q40. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A rod of mass <math> <mi>M</mi> </math> and length <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi></mrow> </math> is suspended at its middle by a wire. It exhibits torsional oscillations. If two masses, each of mass <math><mi>m</mi></math>, are attached at a distance <math> <mrow> <mi>L</mi><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </math> from its centre on both sides, it reduces the oscillation frequency by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>%</mo></math>. The value of ratio <math><mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mrow></math> is close to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>0.17</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>0.77</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>0.57</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>0.37</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>0.37</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let <math><mi>C</mi></math> be the torsional constant of the wire.

<math><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>12</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>.</mo><mi> </mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

After masses are attached,

<math><msup><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>′</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>.</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>12</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mn>0.8</mn><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><msup><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mn>0.64</mn><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0.64</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0.64</mn><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>M</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0.37</mn></math>

</details>

---

### Q41. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle at the end of a spring executes simple harmonic motion with a period <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>, while the corresponding period for another spring is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. If the period of oscillation with the two springs in series is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math>, then -

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>T</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>T</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#8195;</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></msqrt><mo>&#8195;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

when springs are in series then

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

squaring and adding (i) and (ii) we get

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfrac><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfrac><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q42. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is attached to three identical springs <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> each of force constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math> a shown in figure. If the particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is pushed slightly against the spring <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and released then the time period of oscillations is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/103705.JPG" style="width: 150px; height: 164px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/103705-1.JPG" style="width: 150px; height: 162px;" />

When the particle of mass <math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> at O is pushed by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></math> in the direction of A, the spring A will be compressed by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></math> while spring B and C will be stretched by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>ycos</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo></math>. So that the total restoring force on the mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> along OA.

<math><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi>net</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mi>cos</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mi>cos</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>ky</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>ky</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi><mi>cos</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>ky</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>ycos</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo></mrow></mfenced><mi>cos</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>ky</mi></math>

Also <math><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi>net</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></math><p><math><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>ky</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q43. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A uniform rod of length <math> <mtext>L</mtext> </math> and mass <math> <mtext>M</mtext> </math> is pivoted at the centre. Its two ends are attached to two springs of equal spring constant <math> <mtext>k</mtext></math>. The rod is gently pushed through a small angle <math> <mrow> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math> in one direction and released. The frequency of oscillation is (the springs are fixed to the walls)

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/23827.PNG" style="width: 168px; height: 147px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>&pi;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>&pi;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>1</mn>   <mrow>    <mn>2</mn><mi>&#x03C0;</mi></mrow>  </mfrac>  <msqrt>   <mrow>    <mfrac>     <mrow>      <mn>3</mn><mtext>k</mtext></mrow>     <mtext>M</mtext>    </mfrac>    </mrow>  </msqrt>  </mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>6</mn> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>6</mn> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Consider the situation below

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/23827-S.PNG" style="width: 200px; height: 202px;" />

From the above diagram

        <math> <mrow> <mi>θ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>l</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mo>/</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒    </mo> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>L</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

In the above diagram the rod is displaced through an angle <math> <mrow> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math> as in above diagram

Restoring torque <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>kx</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>×</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>L</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>kxL</mtext></mrow></math>
angular acceleration, 
<math><mrow><mo>⇒</mo><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"> <mrow> <mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>×</mo><mfrac> <mi>L</mi> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mi>θ</mi><mo>×</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow> <mi>Ι</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> <math> <mo>=</mo><mfenced> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>6</mn><mtext>k</mtext></mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced><mi>θ</mi> </math></mrow></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><msup> <mi>ω</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mi>θ</mi></mrow> </math>

<math> <mi>ω</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>6</mn><mi>K</mi></mrow> <mi>M</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </msqrt> </math>

<math> <mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>6</mn><mi>K</mi></mrow> <mi>M</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </msqrt> </math>

</details>

### Q44. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A wooden cylinder of mass <math> <mrow> <mtext>20 g</mtext></mrow> </math> and area of cross-section <math> <mrow> <mtext>1</mtext><mtext> </mtext><msup> <mrow> <mtext>cm</mtext></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>, having a piece of lead of mass <math> <mrow> <mn>60</mn><mtext> </mtext><mtext>g</mtext></mrow> </math> attached to its bottom, floats in water. The cylinder is depressed and then released. The frequency of oscillations is <!-- MathType@Translator@5@5@MathML2 (namespace attr).tdl@MathML 2.0 (namespace attr)@ --> <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mtext>N</mtext> <mtext>π</mtext> </mfrac> <msup> <mn>s</mn> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></math>. Find the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi></math>. [Neglect the volume of water displaced by the lead piece, take <!-- MathType@Translator@5@5@MathML2 (namespace attr).tdl@MathML 2.0 (namespace attr)@ --> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9.8</mn><mtext> </mtext><msup><mtext>m/s</mtext><mtext>2</mtext></msup></math>, density of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ρ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>]<!-- MathType@End@5@5@ -->

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1.75**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Suppose that the loaded wooden block sinks upto a height h. Then,

weight of water displaced by the block

= weight of the block with lead

If a is area of cross-section of the block and σ, the density of water, then

<math> <mrow> <mi>a</mi> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mi>σ</mi> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>g</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>or</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>a</mi> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mi>σ</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>×</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>8</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>cm</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mo>∵</mo> <mtext>density of water</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn><mtext>g</mtext><msup><mtext>cm</mtext> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

Now for small displacement '<math> <mi>x</mi></math>' from equilibrium

<math> <mrow> <mi>σ</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>a</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>h</mi></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>−</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>-(</mo> <mrow> <mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mi>σ</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>-(</mo> <mrow> <mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mi>A=-</mi><mfrac> <mrow><mi>σ</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>a</mi></mrow> <mrow> <mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow> </mfrac> <mi>x</mi></mrow> </math>

<math> <mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>g</mi> <mi>h</mi> </mfrac></mrow></msqrt></math>

<math> <mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>980</mn></mrow> <mrow> <mn>80</mn></mrow> </mfrac></mrow></msqrt></math> <!-- MathType@Translator@5@5@MathML2 (namespace attr).tdl@MathML 2.0 (namespace attr)@ --> <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1.75</mn></mrow> <mi>π</mi> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>s</mtext> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> <!-- MathType@End@5@5@ -->
 </p>

</details>

### Q45. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A uniform cylinder of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> and mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> having cross-sectional area <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is suspended, with its length vertical from a fixed point by a massless spring such that it is half-submerged in a liquid of density <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">ρ</mi></math> at the equilibrium position. When the cylinder is given a small downward push and released it starts oscillating vertically with a small amplitude. If the force constant of the spring is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math>, the frequency of oscillation of the cylinder is

<u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0007490_AnsExp2_1.jpg" style="height: 220px; width: 141px;" /></strong></u>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>Aρg</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mtext>k</mtext><mo>+</mo><mtext>A</mtext><mi>&#961;</mi><mtext>g</mtext></mrow><mtext>M</mtext></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>&#961;gL</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>Aρg</mi></mrow><mi>Aρg</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mtext>k</mtext><mo>+</mo><mtext>A</mtext><mi>&#961;</mi><mtext>g</mtext></mrow><mtext>M</mtext></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

When cylinder is displaced by an amount x from its meanposition, spring force and upthrust both will increase. Hence,
Net restoring force = extra spring force + extra upthrust  

Or   <math><mrow> <mtext>F</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>kx</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>Ax</mtext> <mi>ρ</mi> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>
 
Or <math> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>k</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mi>ρ</mi> <mtext>Ag</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>x</mtext> </mrow> </math>

Now, <math> <mrow> <mtext>f</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfenced close="|" open="|" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

          <math><mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>k</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mi>ρ</mi> <mtext>Ag</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

<u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0007490_AnsExp2_1.jpg" /></strong></u>

</details>

---

### Q46. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Time period of a spring-mass system is <math><mi>T</mi></math>. If this spring is cut into two parts whose lengths are in the ratio 1:3 and the same mass is attached to the longer part, the new time period will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mi> </mi><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mi>&#960;</mi> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

When spring is cut in the ratio 1 : 3, the force constant of the longer part is <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mtext>T'</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mtext>m</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mtext>k</mtext></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mtext>T</mtext></math>

</details>

---

### Q47. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A mass <math><mi>M</mi></math> is suspended from a spring of negligible mass. The spring is pulled a little and then released so that the mass executes SHM of time period <math><mi>T</mi></math>. If the mass is increased by <math><mi>m</mi></math>, the time period become <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>. Then the ratio of <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow><mrow><mn>25</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math><mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math> when mass is increased by <math><mi>m</mi></math> then                               ...(i)

                          <math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo></math>                          <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>                                                               ...(ii)

Dividing Eq. (i) by Eq. (ii), we get

                               <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

                               <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>25</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo></math>             <math><mn>9</mn><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>M</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo></math>                       <math><mn>16</mn><mi>M</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>m</mi></math>

                                 <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>
